- **sch-insert**: Use when new work is discovered mid-run and must land at the right position in task.md without renumbering anything. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-insert))
- **sch-inspect**: Inspects and explains KiCad 10 schematics from native .kicad_sch files: components, pins, nets, labels, buses, hierarchical sheets, and sheet instances. Use when the user asks to inspect, trace, or explain schematic circuits, connections, or functional blocks. Targeted, read-only; it extracts evidence and does not judge the design. ([Natchpai/Hardware-Copilot](https://github.com/Natchpai/Hardware-Copilot/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/sch-inspect))
- **sch-learn**: SCH-LOOP v2 structured learning from rejections, regressions, Council predictions, and outcomes. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-learn))
- **sch-learn**: Distill reusable, generalizable lessons from a finished SCH Loop task or engagement into the pack's knowledge base, so the next run starts ahead of zero. Reads findings + reviewer notes; appends deduped rules; never stores client/target-specific data. Called by sch-run/sch-ship or run directly with --project. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/skills/sch-learn))
- **sch-onboard**: Adopt an EXISTING repository into SCH Loop. Reads the code, runs its own build and tests, finds broken and unused code with real tools, and reports what it understood, what is missing, and what it could not verify — then asks exactly three questions and hands off to brainstorming. Use for any repo that already has code. Interactive. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/skills/sch-onboard))
- **sch-plan**: Use when an approved architecture needs slicing into phases and vertical slices before tickets are written. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-plan))
- **sch-plan**: Turn a project's PRD (dev) or SCOPE + pack phases (offensive) into a task queue. INTERACTIVE by default — plays back what it understood, gets the operator's agreement on the phase map, then decomposes each phase into small verifiable tasks. The loop also calls it unattended to fold new inbox items. Always operates on one --project. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/skills/sch-plan))
- **sch-prd**: Use when an approved brainstorm needs a PRD with numbered requirements before any architecture or planning work. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-prd))
- **sch-resume**: SCH-LOOP Resume phase. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-resume))
- **sch-review**: Reviews an electronic schematic or a focused circuit block for engineering correctness, risks, missing protection, component suitability and design assumptions. Use when the user asks for a schematic review, a circuit design check, a power or signal-path review, or an assessment of a KiCad schematic before PCB layout. Not for looking up schematic data only. ([Natchpai/Hardware-Copilot](https://github.com/Natchpai/Hardware-Copilot/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/sch-review))
- **sch-review**: SCH-LOOP Review phase. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-review))
- **sch-review**: Review one SCH Loop task's branch against its acceptance criteria with fresh context, then return approved or changes with must-fix findings. Called by sch-run as a subagent; never merges, never pushes. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/skills/sch-review))
- **sch-run**: Use when tickets exist and the user says go — starts the code-owned dispatch loop that runs every ready ticket and stops only when a human is needed. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-run))
- **sch-run**: The SCH Loop engine, one project per loop. One pass fully completes one task per its pack — build/execute, validate, review with fresh context, complete (merge or log finding), grow the queue, and deliver when done. Enforces the offensive scope gate. Designed for /loop; run as /loop 15m /sch-run --project &lt;id&gt;. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/skills/sch-run))
- **sch-setup**: Use when onboarding a repository to SCH-LOOP, or when roles.json, the fence hooks, or the CLAUDE.md managed block need to be (re)written. User-invoked only. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-setup))
- **sch-ship**: Deliver a SCH Loop project when its queue is drained and its contract is met — deploy (app), package/publish (tool), or produce the CERT-In / engagement report (offensive). Called by sch-run's deliver-check; gated so it never ships an incomplete contract. Operates on one --project. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/skills/sch-ship))
- **sch-ship**: Use when a phase has passed verification and the work should leave this machine — re-runs the release gates and opens the pull request. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-ship))
- **sch-spec**: SCH-LOOP Specification phase. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-spec))
- **sch-spec**: The single entry point to start any SCH Loop work. For a pentest CR just give the CR ref + target(s) — it records the asset under the client's standing authorization, arms the project, and plans it. For app/tool dev it interviews and writes a PRD. After this, the only other thing to run is the loop. Interactive. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/skills/sch-spec))
- **sch-status**: SCH-LOOP Status phase. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-status))
- **sch-tickets**: Use when an approved plan must become the executable queue — writes task.md and one JSON per ticket. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-tickets))
- **sch-verify**: Use when a phase claims to be finished — checks the codebase actually delivers the phase goal, working backwards from the goal rather than forwards from the tickets. ([shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP](https://github.com/shekharcharles/SCH-LOOP/tree/HEAD/sdlc/engine/skills/sch-verify))
- **sch2dsl**: Parse Altium Designer .SchDoc files into verified SCH-DSL and use the generated schematic document to answer component, pin, net, power, CAN, signal-path, and topology questions. Use whenever a user provides an Altium schematic, asks for netlist extraction, pin connectivity, circuit-topology interpretation, or wants an LLM-readable schematic representation. ([wuwuzhazha/sch2dsl](https://github.com/wuwuzhazha/sch2dsl/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/sch2dsl) · ★1)
- **sch2kicad**: Convert a schematic image or a guitar-pedal build document into a KiCad 10 schematic in the user's Pedal Division house style — verified pin for pin, footprints assigned, ready for PCB layout — and save its functional blocks (tone stack, gain stage, buffer...) as their own schematics. Use whenever the user wants to convert, redraw, copy or import a schematic from a PNG/JPG, a PDF build doc (PedalPCB, Aion FX, Parasit Studio, RunOffGroove...) or a Chuck's Boneyard post from the PedalPCB forum into KiCad; wants to copy a circuit block out of a published schematic; wants a schematic from a strip… ([drlholloway/sch2kicad](https://github.com/drlholloway/sch2kicad/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/sch2kicad))
- **sch_organize**: Reorganise a KiCad schematic through the Konnect MCP into independent sub-modules. Every sheet is split into modules, each holding everything one function needs (chip, its resistors/capacitors and other passives, connectors/interfaces; non-chip parts whose nets connect are kept together); each module is laid out in its own region of the page, the regions are separated by blue dashed lines (KiCad "Draw Lines") and named, and modules connect to each other only through net labels and power symbols. Use when the user asks to organise, tidy, partition, modularise or clean up a schematic, or to gro… ([liuweiseu/agent-plugins](https://github.com/liuweiseu/agent-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pcb-design/skills/sch_organize))
